Everything else is\n    identical SQL, so it is written once here rather than twice in the engines.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    #: Spelled by the DB-API driver, not by this class's callers.\n    paramstyle = \"qmark\"\n    returning = False\n\n    def connect(self) -> Any:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def close(self) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def placeholder(self, index: int) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Render parameter ``index`` (0-based) in this engine's spelling.\"\"\"\n        return \"?\" if self.paramstyle == \"qmark\" else \"%s\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def quote(identifier: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Quote a declared identifier. offset)}\"\n            for offset, name in enumerate(names)\n        )\n\n    def select(self, table: str, columns: Sequence[str], predicate: Sequence[str]) -> str:\n        selected = \", self.returning and key:\n            statement += f\" RETURNING {self.quote(key)}\"\n        return statement\n\n    def update(self, table: str, columns: Sequence[str], predicate: Sequence[str]) -> return statement\n\n    def inserted_key(self, cursor: Any) -> Any:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n\nclass TableAccess:\n    \"\"\"The methods one table Connection\n    handling is internal and may change without affecting any caller.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, dsn: str, schema: Schema, *, driver: Driver | None = None) -> None:\n        self._schema = schema\n        self._driver = driver if driver is not None else open_driver(dsn)\n        grouped = schema.by_table()\n        for table in schema.tables:\n            setattr(self, table.name, TableAccess(table, grouped.get(table.name, ()), self))\n\n    @property\n    def driver(self) -> Driver:\n        return self._driver\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        self._driver.close()\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> Database:\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, *_: object) -> None:\n        self.close()\n\n    def execute_script(self, sql: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Apply generated DDL. Any,\n        connection: Any,\n        names: list[str],\n        values: list[Any],\n    ) -> Any:\n        verb = operation.verb\n        table = operation.table\n        if verb in (\"get\", \"list\"):\n            columns = operation.columns or self._columns(table)\n            cursor.execute(self._driver.select(table, columns, names), values)\n            rows = cursor.fetchall() if verb == \"list\" else cursor.fetchmany(1)\n            built = [self._build(operation, columns, row) for row in rows]\n            return built if verb == \"list\" else (built[0] if built else None)\n cursor.execute(self._driver.update(table, assigned, [key]), ordered)\n            connection.commit()\n            return cursor.rowcount\n        if verb == \"delete\":\n            cursor.execute(self._driver.delete(table, names), values)\n            connection.commit()\n            return cursor.rowcount\n        raise UnknownOperation(f\"{operation.identifier} names unsupported verb {verb!r}\")\n\n    def _columns(self, table: str) -> tuple[str, ...]:\n        for declared in self._schema.tables:\n            if declared.name == table:\n                return operation.row(**{\n            name: value for name, value in zip(columns, row, strict=False) if name in accepted\n        })\n\n\ndef statements(sql: str) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Split a DDL script into statements.\n\n    Comment lines are dropped and a semicolon inside a quoted literal does not end a statement —\n    a ``DEFAULT \\'a;b\\'`` would otherwise be cut in half, and the halves are not valid SQL.\n    \"\"\"\n    body = \"\\n\".join(line for line in sql.splitlines() if not line.lstrip().startswith(\"--\"))\n    parts: list[str] = []\n    current: list[str] = []\n    quoted = False\n It parses the same declaration PostgreSQL does and\nignores what it does not enforce — ``VARCHAR(n)`` length in particular — so one rendered schema\nserves both engines and nothing is stripped for one of them.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport sqlite3\nimport threading\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\n\ntry:\n    from .db import Driver\nexcept ImportError:  # flat layout\n    from db import Driver\n\n__all__ = [\"SqliteDriver\"]\n\n\nclass SqliteDriver(Driver):\n    \"\"\"One connection per thread, opened on first use in that thread.\n\n    A ``sqlite3.Connection`` belongs to the thread that created it and raises ``ProgrammingError``\n    when used from another, so a single connection opened during construction is valid in no web\n    request at all. This is not concurrency: queries stay sequential within a workflow, and the\n    rule only stops one connection being shared across unrelated ones.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    paramstyle = \"qmark\"\n    returning = False\n\n    def __init__(self, dsn: str) -> None:\n        self._path = _path_from(dsn)\n        self._local = threading.local()\n\n    def connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:\n        connection = getattr(self._local, \"connection\", None)\n        if connection is None:\n            if self._path != \":memory:\":\n                Path(self._path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            connection = sqlite3.connect(self._path)\n            connection.execute(\"PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL\")\n            connection.execute(\"PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON\")\n            self._local.connection = connection\n        return connection\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        connection = getattr(self._local, \"connection\", None)\n        if connection is not None:\n            connection.close()\n            self._local.connection = None\n\n    def inserted_key(self, cursor: Any) -> Any:\n        return cursor.lastrowid\n\n\ndef _path_from(dsn: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Read a filesystem path out of a DSN, accepting a bare path as well as a URL.\"\"\"\n    if \"://\" not in dsn:\n        return dsn or \":memory:\"\n    _, _, remainder = dsn.partition(\"://\")\n    return remainder or \":memory:\"\n== persistence/testing.py\n\"\"\"Generated by Drydock. This is not concurrency: queries stay sequential within a workflow, and the\n    rule only stops one connection being shared across unrelated ones.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    paramstyle = \"qmark\"\n    returning = False\n\n    def __init__(self, dsn: str) -> None:\n        self._path = _path_from(dsn)\n        self._local = threading.local()\n\n    def connect(self) -> sqlite3.Connection:\n        connection = getattr(self._local, \"connection\", None)\n        if connection is None:\n            if self._path != \":memory:\":\n                Path(self._path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            connection = sqlite3.connect(self._path)\n            connection.execute(\"PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL\")\n            connection.execute(\"PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON\")\n            self._local.connection = connection\n        return connection\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        connection = getattr(self._local, \"connection\", None)\n        if connection is not None:\n            connection.close()\n            self._local.connection = None\n\n    def inserted_key(self, cursor: Any) -> Any:\n        return cursor.lastrowid\n\n\ndef _path_from(dsn: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Read a filesystem path out of a DSN, accepting a bare path as well as a URL.\"\"\"\n    if \"://\" not in dsn:\n        return dsn or \":memory:\"\n    _, _, remainder = dsn.partition(\"://\")\n    return remainder or \":memory:\"\n== persistence/testing.py\n\"\"\"Generated by Drydock.
